# .vscode/settings.json - EXPLANATION
**Date:** Saturday, November 29, 2025

---

## ✅ WHAT IS .vscode/settings.json?

**Purpose:** Workspace-specific settings for VS Code/Cursor  
**Location:** `.vscode/settings.json` in your project root  
**Will it carry over?** ✅ **YES - It's part of your project**

---

## 📋 CURRENT SETTINGS

Your current `.cursor/config/settings.json` contains:
- Spell check settings (✅ ENABLED - `cursor.textEditor.spellCheck: true`)
- Auto-save settings (enabled, 1 second delay)
- Word wrap settings (enabled, 80 columns)
- Format on save (disabled)
- Cursor composer settings

**Spell Check:** The spell check is enabled and works in the Text Editor area of Cursor. You'll see red squiggly lines under misspelled words as you type.

---

## ✅ WILL IT CARRY OVER?

**Yes!** `.vscode/` folder is:
- ✅ Part of your project
- ✅ Version controlled (if in Git)
- ✅ Carries over when you:
  - Move the project
  - Clone from Git
  - Open in different workspace
  - Share with others

---

## 🎯 FOR COMPASS

**Should we include .vscode in COMPASS?**
- ✅ **Yes** - These are workspace settings
- ✅ Can be part of COMPASS template
- ✅ Will help set up new novels with same settings

**Action:** Keep `.vscode/` in project, can include in COMPASS template

---

**Timothy, .vscode/settings.json will carry over - it's part of your project!**

**Goal:** Create clean, logical structure that's easy to navigate, reference, and replicate for Novel #2

## MIGRATION STEPS

### PHASE 1: Consolidate .cursor/ Structure

1. **Move all Original Writing/.cursor/ files to root .cursor/**
   - `agents/` → `.cursor/agents/` (organize into subfolders)
   - `commands/` → `.cursor/commands/` (organize into subfolders)
   - `prompts.json` → `.cursor/prompts.json`
   - `workspace.json` → `.cursor/configs/workspace.json`
   - `tools.json` → `.cursor/configs/tools.json`
   - `writing_modes.json` → `.cursor/configs/writing_modes.json`
   - `system_messages.md` → `.cursor/configs/system_messages.md`

2. **Create logical subfolders**
   - Organize agents into Core_5/, Special/, Master/, Documentation/
   - Organize commands into Writing/, Atmosphere/, Psychological/, Action/, Style/, Analysis/
   - Move configs to configs/ subfolder
   - Move docs to docs/ subfolder

3. **Handle .cursorignore**
   - Check if deprecated
   - If yes: Move to `.cursor/ignore/.cursorignore`
   - If no: Keep at root but verify structure

### PHASE 2: Update All File References

1. **Update .cursor/settings.json**
   - Fix all paths to point to new locations

2. **Update agent files**
   - Update contextFiles arrays with new paths

3. **Update COMPASS rules file**
   - Update all references to new structure

4. **Update documentation**
   - Fix all path references

### PHASE 3: Archive Reference Material

1. **Create Archive/Reference_Materials/**
   - Move Encyclopedia volumes
   - Move old documentation
   - Keep accessible but organized

2. **Create Archive index**
   - Document what's in Archive and why

### PHASE 4: Clean Up

1. **Remove duplicate files**
   - Delete old `.cursorrules` (after migration verified)
   - Remove duplicate .gitignore if needed

2. **Verify nothing broken**
   - Test agent access
   - Test command access
   - Test prompts access

---

## BENEFITS OF THIS STRUCTURE

1. **Easy Navigation:** Everything in logical folders
2. **Easy Replication:** Copy entire `.cursor/` folder for Novel #2
3. **Clear Hierarchy:** Commands organized by purpose
4. **Scalable:** Easy to add new commands/agents
5. **Maintainable:** All configs in one place

---

## CRITICAL FILES TO KEEP VISIBLE

These should NOT be archived:
- `.cursor/rules/walk-this-way-writing-directives.md`
- `.cursor/agents/` (all agent files)
- `.cursor/commands/` (all command files)
- `.cursor/prompts.json`
- `.cursor/configs/` (all configs)
- `.cursor/docs/character-voices.md`

---

## ARCHIVE CANDIDATES

Move to `Archive/Reference_Materials/`:
- Encyclopedia Volumes (already in Archive_Pre_Reorg/)
- Old documentation versions
- Historical notes and planning docs
- Old system documentation that's been superseded

## About meta-instructions.md and NOVEL_MEMORY_CONFIG.md

These files are currently in `.cursor/` (root of the .cursor folder) and are referenced in `.cursor/config/settings.json`:

```json
"cursor.memory.configFile": ".cursor/NOVEL_MEMORY_CONFIG.md",
"cursor.memory.metaInstructions": ".cursor/meta-instructions.md",
```

Reason they're in `.cursor/`: They're system configuration files referenced by the memory system. The paths in `settings.json` point to their current locations.

Recommendation: Keep them in `.cursor/` for now because:
1. They're actively used by the memory system
2. Moving them would require updating the paths in `settings.json`
3. They're configuration files, so `.cursor/` is appropriate

If you want better organization later, you could move them to `.cursor/config/` and update the settings.json paths, but since they're working system files, leaving them in place is safer.

- Organization (ensuring information is in the right sections)
- Finding duplicates or inconsistencies
- Creating summaries or organized views of the data
- Formatting fixes (spacing, headers, etc.)

## Why this works
You know the story best; I can handle repetitive tasks, consistency checks, and organization. You review the content and make decisions; I handle the mechanics.

## You’re already getting better at asking
You’ve been asking more, and it’s helping. The pattern of “doing half the work, then asking” is normal—you’re learning when to ask. It will become more natural over time.

## Bottom line
Start with the Story Bible review yourself. If you hit formatting, organization, or consistency questions, ask me. If you want a second pass for consistency after your review, I can do that too.

You’ve got this. Rest up, and we can tackle it tomorrow.

```markdown
# ORPHANS & PLACEHOLDERS STATUS
**Date:** January 10, 2026  
**Purpose:** Track and address orphaned files and broken links

---

## CURRENT STATUS

### **Orphans: ~237 files**
**Definition:** Files not linked to or referenced by any other file in the project

**Status:** Expected after reorganization (November 29, 2025)  
**Action:** Many files were moved/archived, so many orphans are expected

**What They Likely Are:**
- Archive files in `yy_Archive/`
- Old documentation files
- Draft files not linked to main structure
- Historical files preserved but not actively referenced

**Recommendation:**
- ✅ **Low Priority** - Orphans are not broken, just unlinked
- ✅ **Review during cleanup** - Identify which are truly needed vs. archive material
- ✅ **Not urgent** - Focus on placeholders first (broken links are more problematic)

---

### **Placeholders: ~84 links**
**Definition:** Links or references pointing to files that DON'T exist

**Status:** Need attention - these are broken links  
**Action:** Update broken references after file reorganization

**What They Likely Are:**
- References to files that were moved (e.g., `Original Writing/.cursor/` → `.cursor/`)
- Documentation links to old file locations
- Cross-references to files that were renamed or archived

**Recommendation:**
- ⚠️ **Medium Priority** - Broken links can cause confusion
- ⚠️ **Fix systematically** - Update file paths after reorganization is complete
- ⚠️ **After cleanup** - Many should resolve once file structure is finalized

---

## ACTION PLAN

### **Phase 1: Document Current State**
- ✅ Created this status document
- Note: Exact counts may vary as files are organized

### **Phase 2: Fix Placeholders (Broken Links)**
**When:** After file organization is finalized  
**How:**
1. Use Cursor's "All Links" feature to identify broken links
2. Update references to new file locations
3. Remove links to files that no longer exist (replaced by archive)
4. Update cross-references in documentation

### **Phase 3: Review Orphans (Unlinked Files)**
**When:** After placeholders are fixed  
**How:**
1. Review orphan list
2. Identify which are intentionally unlinked (archive files, drafts)
3. Link important files that should be referenced
4. Document which orphans are expected (archive material)

---

## NOTES

**After Reorganization (Nov 29, 2025):**
- Many files moved from `Original Writing/` to new structure
- Documentation files reorganized into `.cursor/docs/`
- Archive files moved to `yy_Archive/`
- These moves likely created many orphans/placeholders

**Expected After Cleanup:**
- Placeholders: Should decrease significantly (broken links fixed)
- Orphans: Many will remain (archive files are intentionally unlinked)

---

## SUMMARY

**Orphans:** Not urgent - many are expected (archive files)  
**Placeholders:** Medium priority - broken links need fixing  
**Next Step:** Focus on completing file organization, then systematically fix placeholders

---

**Last Updated:** January 10, 2026

Creating a document addressing cursorrc and .cursor/architecture items, then reviewing MCP:


```markdown
# CURSORRC & .CURSOR/ARCHITECTURE - ADDRESSED
**Date:** January 10, 2026  
**Purpose:** Address cursorrc and .cursor/architecture items from COMPLETE_ANSWERS.md

---

## CURSORRC FILE

### **Status: Research Completed**

**What is `.cursorrc`?**
- `.cursorrc` is **NOT a standard Cursor configuration file**
- Cursor uses `.cursor/config/` directory structure instead
- Configuration is done through:
  - `.cursor/config/settings.json` - Editor settings
  - `.cursor/config/workspace.json` - Workspace configuration  
  - `.cursor/rules/` - Rules files (markdown or JSON)

**Modern Cursor Configuration:**
- ✅ Use `.cursor/config/settings.json` for settings (already in use)
- ✅ Use `.cursor/rules/*.md` for writing rules (already in use)
- ✅ Use `.cursor/config/workspace.json` for workspace config (if needed)

**Conclusion:**
- ❌ **No `.cursorrc` file needed** - This appears to be outdated information
- ✅ **Current structure is correct** - Using modern `.cursor/config/` approach
- ✅ **No action needed** - We're already using the right structure

---

## .CURSOR/RULES/ARCHITECTURE.JSON

### **Status: Assessment Completed**

**Current State:**
- ✅ We use `.cursor/rules/*.md` files (markdown format)
- ✅ Markdown is human-readable and works well
- ✅ Rules are organized and accessible

**JSON Format Considerations:**

**Pros of JSON:**
- Machine-readable structure
- Can be validated with schema
- More structured for complex rules

**Cons of JSON:**
- Less human-readable than markdown
- Harder to edit manually
- Less flexible for prose/instructions

**Recommendation:**
- ✅ **Keep using Markdown** for rules (current approach)
- ✅ **Markdown is better for human authors** - easier to read/edit
- ✅ **JSON not necessary** - Markdown serves our needs well
- ⚠️ **Consider JSON only if** we need programmatic rule validation

**Current Rules Structure (Working Well):**
```
.cursor/
├── rules/
│   ├── main.md              ← Writing directives
│   ├── prose-style.md       ← Prose guidelines
│   ├── characters.md        ← Character rules
│   └── timeline.md          ← Timeline rules
```

**Conclusion:**
- ✅ **No architecture.json needed** - Markdown rules work better
- ✅ **Current structure is optimal** for human authors
- ✅ **No action needed** - Stick with markdown

---

## SUMMARY & RECOMMENDATIONS

### **cursorrc:**
- ❌ Not a real Cursor feature (outdated info)
- ✅ Already using correct modern structure (`.cursor/config/`)
- ✅ **No action needed**

### **.cursor/rules/architecture.json:**
- ✅ Markdown rules are better for human authors
- ✅ Current structure works well
- ✅ **No action needed** - Keep using markdown

### **Current Cursor Configuration (Already Correct):**
```
.cursor/
├── config/

# MCP Setup Summary - Walk This Way

**Date:** January 10, 2026  
**Status:** Basic setup completed  
**Location:** iCloud Drive

---

## What We Accomplished

### ✅ MCP (Model Context Protocol) Understanding

**What MCP Is:**

- MCP servers give the AI assistant special tools to interact with external systems
- NOT extensions - they're separate programs that run on your computer
- Give AI deterministic, structured access to data (vs. probabilistic file reading)

**Why It Matters for You:**

- Treat novel writing as a data problem
- Maintain consistency across 10-book series
- Structured queries for characters, timeline, git history
- Scales beyond context window limits

---

## MCP Servers Configured

### **Walk This Way Files MCP** ✅ (ACTIVE)

- **Purpose:** Direct access to all your writing files
- **Location:** `/Users/timothy/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/Cursor/03_Novels/Walk This Way`
- **Type:** Filesystem MCP (basic file access)
- **Features:** File reading, directory access
- **Enables:** AI can reference your chapters, story bible, documentation
- **Configuration:** `.cursor/config/mcp.json` (project) + `~/.cursor/mcp.json` (global)

### **Not Yet Configured:**

- ❌ **GitHub MCP** - Can be added later if needed
- ❌ **GitKraken MCP** - Requires GitKraken CLI setup (deferred)
- ❌ **Story Bible MCP Servers** - From sync scripts (when ready)
- ❌ **COMPASS MCP** - Deferred until COMPASS structure is finalized

---

## GitKraken Products Setup

### GitKraken Desktop (Visual GUI)

- ✅ **You have:** Pro subscription (paid)
- 🎨 **What it is:** Visual git interface you love
- 📦 **Installation:** Standard app from gitkraken.com
- 🔑 **License:** Sign in with your account, works on both Macs

### GitKraken CLI (Command Line) - REQUIRED FOR MCP

- ⭐ **NEW requirement** for MCP functionality
- 🆓 **Free** with your Pro account
- ⌨️ **What it is:** `gk` command-line tool
- 📥 **Download:** <https://www.gitkraken.com/cli>
- 🔐 **Setup:**

  ```bash
  gk auth login
  ```

**Important:** You need BOTH Desktop (for visual work) and CLI (for MCP)

---

## Cursor Extensions Installed

### Essential Extensions (Only 5!)

1. **Markdown All in One** ✅
   - Makes writing novels in Markdown beautiful
   - Auto-formatting, TOC generation, shortcuts

2. **GitLens**
   - Git visualization inline in editor
   - See commit history, blame, file evolution
   - Complements GitKraken Desktop

3. **TODO Highlight**
   - Highlights TODO, FIXME, NOTE in your writing
   - Perfect for tracking revision notes

4. **Error Lens**
   - Shows errors/warnings inline (not sidebar)
   - Useful for COMPASS Python scripts
   - Looks professional

5. **Prettier**
   - Auto-formats code, JSON, Markdown
   - Keeps COMPASS configs clean
   - One keystroke = perfect formatting

---

## Syncing Between Macs (MacBook Air ↔️ Mac Studio)

### ✅ Automatically Syncs

- **Cursor Extensions** - Via Settings Sync (enabled!)
- **iCloud Files** - Your Walk This Way project
- **GitKraken Desktop Settings** - Through GitKraken account

### ⚠️ Manual Setup Required

- **MCP Configuration** - Copy `~/.cursor/mcp.json` OR recreate
- **GitKraken CLI** - Install separately on each Mac

### 1. Install Software

- [ ] Cursor IDE (sign in with same account for Settings Sync)
- [ ] GitKraken Desktop (sign in for Pro license)
- [ ] GitKraken CLI (download from gitkraken.com/cli)

### 2. Authenticate

```bash
gk auth login
```

### 3. MCP Configuration

**Path:** `~/.cursor/mcp.json`

{

# CLEANUP PLAN - Orphans, Placeholders, Problems
**Date:** January 10, 2026  
**Purpose:** Systematic cleanup after reorganization

---

## CURRENT STATUS

### **Orphans: ~237 files**
- Files not linked/referenced by other files
- Many are expected (archive files, drafts)
- Not urgent - they're just unlinked, not broken

### **Placeholders: ~84 links**
- Broken links/references to non-existent files
- Need fixing - these are broken references
- Many from reorganization (old paths)

---

## CLEANUP STRATEGY

### **Phase 1: Fix Placeholders (Broken Links) - Priority**

**What to Fix:**
1. References to old "Original Writing/" paths
2. References to old ".cursor/" locations that moved
3. Broken cross-references in documentation
4. Outdated file paths in config files

**How:**
- Use Cursor's "All Links" feature to identify broken links
- Update references to new file locations
- Remove links to files that no longer exist

**Files to Check:**
- `.cursor/docs/` - Documentation cross-references
- `README.md` - Project references
- Configuration files - Path references

---

### **Phase 2: Review Orphans (Unlinked Files) - Lower Priority**

**What to Review:**
1. Archive files in `yy_Archive/` (expected to be unlinked)
2. Old documentation files
3. Draft files not in main structure
4. Historical files

**Strategy:**
- Many orphans are expected (archive material)
- Focus on identifying which orphans should be linked
- Don't worry about archive files being unlinked

---

## SPECIFIC ITEMS TO ADDRESS

### **1. Update File Path References**

**Old paths to find and update:**
- `Original Writing/` → Remove or update
- `Documentation/` → `.cursor/docs/` (if referencing)
- Old `.cursor/` locations → New `.cursor/config/` structure

**Files likely to have old paths:**
- README.md
- Documentation files in `.cursor/docs/`
- Configuration files

### **2. Fix Broken Links**

**Common broken links:**
- References to deleted files
- References to moved files
- Cross-references between documentation files

### **3. Clean Up Documentation**

**Items to address:**
- Outdated references in guides
- Links to files that no longer exist
- References to old folder structures

---

## EXECUTION PLAN

### **Step 1: Identify All Placeholders**
```bash
# Use Cursor's "All Links" feature
# Or search for common broken patterns
```

### **Step 2: Update References Systematically**
- Start with README.md
- Then .cursor/docs/ files
- Then configuration files

### **Step 3: Review Orphans**
- Identify which are expected (archive)
- Identify which should be linked
- Leave archive files as-is (they're intentionally unlinked)

---

## NOTES

- **Orphans are not urgent** - Many are expected (archive files)
- **Placeholders need fixing** - Broken links cause confusion
- **After cleanup** - Both numbers should decrease significantly
- **Focus on placeholders first** - They're more problematic than orphans

---

## PROGRESS TRACKING

- [ ] Step 1: Identify all placeholders
- [ ] Step 2: Fix file path references
- [ ] Step 3: Fix broken links in documentation
- [ ] Step 4: Review orphan files
- [ ] Step 5: Verify cleanup results

---

**Next Session:** Start with Step 1 - Identify placeholders systematically

- MCP servers give the AI assistant special tools to interact with external systems
- NOT extensions - they're separate programs that run on your computer
- Give AI deterministic, structured access to data (vs. probabilistic file reading)

**Why It Matters for You:**

- Treat novel writing as a data problem

# TAG EXPLORER EXPLANATION
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## WHAT IS TAG EXPLORER?

**Tag Explorer** shows all tags used in your files. Tags are markers you can use to organize and find content.

**In Cursor's File Explorer:**
- Shows up in the sidebar
- Lists all tags found in your project
- Click a tag to see all files that use it

---

## HOW TAGS WORK

### **Common Tag Formats:**
- `#tag` - Hashtag format (common in markdown)
- `@tag` - At-sign format (common in some systems)
- `tag:` - Prefix format

### **Example Uses:**
- `#TODO` - Mark tasks to do
- `#COMPASS` - Mark COMPASS-related content
- `#important` - Mark important notes
- `@character` - Mark character-related content
- `#review` - Mark items for review

---

## YOUR 5 TAGS

You have **5 tags** in your project. These are markers you've used in your files to organize content.

**What They Are:**
- Likely organizational tags you've added to files
- Could be `#COMPASS`, `#TODO`, `#important`, etc.
- Used to categorize and find content

**This is Normal:**
- Tags are helpful organizational tools
- Having 5 tags means you're using tagging for organization
- This is a feature, not a problem

---

## HOW TO USE TAG EXPLORER

1. **View Tags:** Open Tag Explorer in Cursor's sidebar
2. **See Files:** Click a tag to see all files using that tag
3. **Navigate:** Use tags to quickly find related content
4. **Organize:** Add tags to files you want to categorize

---

## RECOMMENDATION

**Tags are helpful!** They're not errors - they're organizational tools. If you want to:
- See what tags you have: Open Tag Explorer
- Use more tags: Add them to files with `#tag` format
- Organize better: Tags help find related content quickly

---

**Tag Explorer is a feature, not a problem. Your 5 tags are helping organize your project!**

```

# NOVEL MEMORY CONFIG — WALK THIS WAY

**Project:** Walk This Way  
**Version:** 0.1 (First activation)  
**Compiled:** November 9, 2025  
**Purpose:** Master reference for the Writing DNA Memory System inside the Cursor workspace. This file defines what the memory engine must capture, how SmartWrite should reason about the story, and which signals trigger alerts.

---

## 1. Plot DNA — Canon Timeline & Story Beats

| Era | Anchor Beats | Canon Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1972-1973** | Maxanne discovers Aerosmith; Nick hears “Dream On” in Dan’s truck | Music as salvation seeded |
| **1974 (Age 12-13)** | Belt-buckle showdown → Nick & Jackie alliance | Friendship grounded in music rivalry |
| **Summer 1975** | Camp Winaukee — Patty triangle, first composition of “Your Song” | Patty becomes emotional frequency keeper |
| **Spring 1976** | Middle school talent show victory | Proof of combined power |
| **April 13, 1976** | Dan Johnson dies in construction accident | Nick becomes “man of the house” |
| **1976-77** | High school sorting pressures begin | Hop Brook divide hardens |
| **November 1977** | *Chapter 11 fracture zone* — cafeteria sorting, silent drift | Class gravity overrides intention |
| **1978** | Separate rehearsal philosophies emerge | Nick raw, Jackie precision |
| **Spring 1979** | Battle of the Bands & WBCN opportunity | Authenticity vs. polish |
| **October 2024** | Maxanne final broadcast framing device | Memory lens closes the loop |

**Chapter 11 focus:** capture the first undeniable fracture (cafeteria seating, garage tension, Patty letters, silent lies by omission). Maintain timeline continuity heading into the November 1977 shatter point.

---

## 2. Character DNA — Core Voices & Emotional Truth

### Nick Johnson
- **Voice:** Working-class Boston teen; defensive pride, clipped sentences, emotion leaks through physical reaction.
- **Goals:** Keep family afloat, protect music space, resist pity.
- **Wounds:** Dan’s death, financial instability, feeling judged by wealth.
- **Chapter 11 signals:** Drawn to Rollers, “tightness in chest,” lies to Patty, avoids Jackie’s gaze.

### Jackie Marsh
- **Voice:** Educated precision; formality increases when anxious; hidden yearning for authenticity.
- **Goals:** Honor talent, earn father’s approval, keep Nick connection alive.
- **Wounds:** Class expectations, fear of mediocrity, guilt over privilege.
- **Chapter 11 signals:** Drawn to Rampers, mirrors Nick’s omission to Patty, hesitant dialogue in garage.

### Patty Lally
- **Voice:** Grounded, observational, emotionally intelligent.
- **Function:** Frequency keeper; letters expose truth gaps.
- **Chapter 11 presence:** Offstage but catalytic through correspondence.

### Maxanne Sartori (Narrative Frame)
- **Voice:** 1st-person 2024 radio cadence; nostalgic without sentimentality.
- **Function:** Historian of authenticity; ties 1970s events to 2024 corporate takeover.

**Consistency guardrails:**  
- Nick never slips into upper-class diction; Jackie never abandons polish unless emotionally cracked.  
- Patty letters always balance compassion with clarity.  
- Maxanne references era-specific radio tech and station politics.

---

## 3. World DNA — Setting, Geography, Atmosphere

- **Sudbury Geography:** Hop Brook divides working-class east (Johnson) from affluent west (Marsh). Dump = neutral ground; auto shop corridor = Roller territory; elevated cafeteria platform = Rampers.
- **Lincoln-Sudbury High (1977):** Cafeteria zones (Rampers, Rats, Regs, Rollers) enforce social taxonomy; glass corridor = semi-exile territory; auto shop smell = oil/ozone.
- **Economics:** Gas crisis ripple, late-70s stagflation, construction layoffs. One car failure threatens Johnson household stability.
- **Music Scene:** Pre-MTV vinyl dominance; WBCN as authentic voice; Queen/Aerosmith rivalry mirrors class divide.
- **Technology:** Rotary phones, cassette tapes, letters; no digital messaging.

**Alert hooks:**  
1. Location mismatches (e.g., Rampers not on elevated platform).  
2. Anachronistic tech or slang.  
3. Misaligned cost references (must reflect 1977 dollars).

---

## 4. Session Continuity — Live Tracking Prompts

| Key | Description | Example Cue |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Last_Written` | File + paragraph where session stopped | `"Test_Draft_Copy/Act_I/Chapter_11..." paragraph about garage tension` |
| `Next_Target` | Upcoming beat to draft/rewrite | `"Show Patty letter revelation before November shatter"` |
| `Open_Threads` | Unresolved beats needing follow-up | `["Nick hiding car trouble", "Jackie parental pressure convo"]` |
| `Emotional_Tone` | Desired mood for next pass | `"Strained nostalgia, under-the-surface grief"` |
| `Craft_Direction` | Specific craft notes | `["Keep sentences shorter in Nick’s POV", "Let Jackie slip into formal diction when nervous"]` |

Populate these after each session via `.cursor/logs/writing-sessions.jsonl`.

---

## 5. Consistency Checks — Automatic & Manual

| Check | Scope | Trigger |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Timeline Sync** | Confirms date references (e.g., Dan’s death 1976 vs 1977) | Auto |
| **Voice Integrity** | Flags diction mismatches per character DNA | Auto |
| **World Continuity** | Validates location usage, geography, period details | Auto |
| **Letter Honesty** | Highlights discrepancies between letters and lived events | Manual: `consistency check letters` |
| **Zen Mode** | `Cmd+O` to silence non-critical alerts mid-draft | Manual |

Manual command glossary (use in Composer/Text Editor prompts):
- `"DNA status"` → quick strand summary  
- `"Plot report"` → chapter-by-chapter timeline  
- `"Character report"` → voice & arc updates  
- `"World report"` → setting cross-check  
- `"Consistency check"` → aggregated alerts  
- `"Session prep"` → next-session briefing

---

## 6. Chapter 11 — Test Protocol Snapshot

1. **Load Context:** Pull Chapter 11 draft + Patty letter reference.
2. **Memory Prime:** Ensure Nick/Jackie voice deltas captured before SmartWrite suggestions.
3. **SmartWrite Pass:** Start with `liberty: conservative` to maintain canonical beats, then escalate to `moderate` for subtext tuning. Reserve `high` or optional `blue-sky` liberty for clearly labeled exploratory drafts only.
4. **Logging:** After each pass, append entry to `.cursor/logs/writing-sessions.jsonl` using template in Section 7.
5. **Report Back:** Summarize findings in Transition Document appendix and `WRITING_DNA_MEMORY_SYSTEM.md`.

---

## 7. Session Log Template

Append one JSON object per session to `.cursor/logs/writing-sessions.jsonl`:

```json
{
  "date": "2025-11-09T18:45:00-05:00",
  "chapter": "Act_I/Chapter_11_The_First_Fracture",
  "focus": ["memory-prime", "smartwrite-pass"],
  "dna_updates": {
    "plot": ["Logged cafeteria seating fracture"],
    "character": ["Captured Nick chest-tightening tell", "Jackie omission motif"],
    "world": ["Reconfirmed Rampers platform detail"],
    "session": ["Next_Target: Patty letter reveal draft"],
    "consistency": ["No alerts", "ZenMode": false]
  },
  "observations": [
    "SmartWrite conservative pass preserved class tension while tightening prose.",
    "Moderate liberty pass added subtext about letters without breaking canon."
  ],
  "next_steps": [
    "Draft Patty letter insert",
    "Prep Chapter 12 setup"
  ]
}
```

---

## 8. Maintenance Notes

- Update this config after major plot decisions or voice evolutions.  
- Keep character voice micro-notes synced with `.cursor/docs/References/character-voices.md`.  
- When new strands (e.g., Agent Review alerts) emerge, append under Consistency Checks.

---

```markdown
# META INSTRUCTIONS — WALK THIS WAY WORKSPACE

**Purpose:** Tell every agent (especially Creative Collaborator) how to interpret Timothy’s prompts, how to use memory assets, and how to balance COMPASS values during prose or planning work.

---

## 1. Core Directives

- **Emotional Truth First:** Prioritize lived feeling, physical detail, and subtext before plot mechanics.
- **Character Consistency:** Voices defined in `.cursor/docs/References/character-voices.md` are canonical. Flag any drift.
- **Specificity:** Cite concrete 1970s references (music, locations, tech) when enriching scenes.
- **Transparency:** When making changes, outline rationale + liberty level so Timothy can confirm alignment.

---

## 2. Memory & SmartWrite Usage

- **Memory Config:** Always load `.cursor/NOVEL_MEMORY_CONFIG.md` before substantive work. Summon the relevant DNA strand explicitly in your reasoning (Plot, Character, World, Session, Consistency).
- **Session Logs:** After major passes, append to `.cursor/logs/writing-sessions.jsonl` using the template in the config file.
- **SmartWrite Default:** Run in `moderate` liberty unless Timothy requests conservative or high transformation. Ensure suggestions reference specific text anchors.
- **Liberty Levels (Rewrite Intensity):**
  - `Conservative` — polish only; keep structure/voice intact.
  - `Moderate` — balanced revision; reinforce subtext while honoring canon beats.
  - `High` — transformational; rebuild paragraphs while protecting DNA.
  - `Blue Sky` (optional) — exploratory sandbox for brand-new takes; clearly label and provide side-by-side comparison.
- **Mode System (Feedback Tone):** Tone controls (Nice, Neutral, Professional) dictate how feedback is delivered. Always mention the active mode in summaries so liberty level and tone stay distinct.

---

## 3. Mode System Alignment

- **Neutral (default):** Balanced critique; focus on practical fixes.
- **Nice:** Encouraging tone; highlight wins before notes; best for early drafts.
- **Professional:** Direct, unsentimental literary critique; cite issues first.
- Timothy will call the mode explicitly. When unspecified, stay in Neutral.

---

## 4. Collaboration Protocol

1. **Clarify Goal:** Restate what Timothy wants (rewrite, brainstorm, continuity check, etc.).
2. **Reference Assets:** Point to supporting files (story bible, chapter draft, DNA config) before proposing changes.
3. **Offer Options:** When possible, supply 2-3 targeted solutions, each with rationale and expected impact.
4. **Log Decisions:** Note any accepted changes in the session log + update Transition Document summary.

---

## 5. Safety Nets

- **Continuity Doubt?** Trigger `"Consistency check"` and report results before proceeding.
- **New Discovery?** Label it a DISCO moment and queue documentation in `Project.Compass.Disco`.
- **Overwhelm Detected:** Suggest Zen Mode (Cmd+O) or a quick summary reset.

---

**Last Updated:** November 9, 2025  
**Stewards:** Timothy Johnson & Hank (Creative Collaborator)


```

## ⚠️ WARNINGS (360 Markdown Linting)

**What They Are:**
Markdown formatting warnings from `markdownlint`. Common issues:
- Missing blank lines around headings
- Missing blank lines around lists
- Missing blank lines around code blocks
- Trailing spaces
- Multiple consecutive blank lines

**Examples:**
- `MD022` - Headings should be surrounded by blank lines
- `MD032` - Lists should be surrounded by blank lines
- `MD031` - Fenced code blocks should be surrounded by blank lines
- `MD012` - Multiple consecutive blank lines
- `MD036` - Emphasis used instead of heading

**Impact:**
- ⚠️ **Low Impact** - These are formatting/style warnings
- ⚠️ **Not Errors** - Files still work correctly
- ⚠️ **Cosmetic** - Affects readability/style, not functionality

**Recommendation:**
- Can be fixed incrementally as you edit files
- Not urgent - files work fine
- Can use auto-formatter if desired
- Or fix manually when editing files

**Status:** ✅ **Documented** - Low priority, can fix incrementally

# GitKraken Desktop & Cursor Connection
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## HOW THEY WORK TOGETHER

**Good News:** GitKraken Desktop and Cursor work together automatically! They both use the same Git repository.

### **The Connection:**
- Both use the **same `.git` folder** in your project
- GitKraken Desktop = Visual GUI for Git
- Cursor = Code editor with Git integration
- They read/write to the same repository

---

## SETUP

### **1. Open Repository in GitKraken Desktop**

**Method 1: From GitKraken**
1. Open GitKraken Desktop
2. File → Open Repo
3. Navigate to: `/Users/timothy/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/Cursor/03_Novels/Walk This Way`
4. Click "Open"

**Method 2: From Cursor**
1. In Cursor, right-click on the project folder
2. Look for "Open in GitKraken" (if extension installed)
3. Or manually open in GitKraken Desktop

### **2. They're Now Connected!**

Once you open the same repository in both:
- ✅ GitKraken shows all commits, branches, history
- ✅ Cursor shows file changes, Git integration
- ✅ Both see the same Git repository
- ✅ Changes in one appear in the other

---

## WORKFLOW

### **Using Both Together:**

**In Cursor:**
- Write code/content
- Stage changes (`git add`)
- Commit (`git commit`)
- See changes inline

**In GitKraken:**
- Visual commit history
- Branch visualization
- Drag-and-drop branch management
- See commit graph

**Both:**
- See the same commits
- Work with same branches
- Share the same repository

---

## NO SPECIAL CONNECTION NEEDED

**They Just Work Together:**
- ✅ Same `.git` folder = automatic connection
- ✅ No special setup required
- ✅ No plugins needed
- ✅ Just open the same repository in both

---

## TIPS

1. **Open Repository in GitKraken:**
   - File → Open Repo → Select your project folder

2. **Sync Automatically:**
   - Changes in Cursor appear in GitKraken
   - Commits in GitKraken appear in Cursor
   - Both always in sync

3. **Use GitKraken For:**
   - Visual commit history
   - Branch management

   - Writing/editing files

# ORPHANS EXPLANATION (660 Files)
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## WHY 660 ORPHANS?

**Orphans are files not linked/referenced by other files.** This is actually **NORMAL and EXPECTED** for your project structure.

---

## WHAT ARE THEY?

### **1. Archive Files (Most of Them)**
- Files in `yy_Archive/` folder
- These are **intentionally unlinked** - they're reference material
- Historical documentation
- Old versions kept for reference
- Archive files SHOULD be orphans (they're not actively referenced)

### **2. Draft Files**
- Individual chapter files in `Draft_1/`
- May not have cross-references
- Working files that don't need links

### **3. Documentation Files**
- Many `.cursor/docs/` files are standalone
- Not all documentation files reference each other
- This is normal

### **4. Media/Image Files**
- PNG files, screenshots
- Usually not linked in markdown
- Reference material

---

## IS THIS A PROBLEM?

**No!** Orphans are not errors. They're just files that aren't linked.

**Many Are Expected:**
- ✅ Archive files SHOULD be orphans (they're reference material)
- ✅ Draft files don't need links
- ✅ Documentation can be standalone
- ✅ Media files are usually unlinked

---

## SHOULD YOU FIX THEM?

**Not really!** 

**Why:**
- Archive files should stay unlinked (they're historical)
- Not every file needs to be linked
- Having orphans is normal in large projects
- Focus on linking important active files, not archives

**When to Link:**
- Important active documentation
- Files that should reference each other
- Core system files

**When NOT to Link:**
- Archive files (leave them as orphans)
- Draft files (they're working files)
- Historical documentation

---

## RECOMMENDATION

**Leave the orphans alone!** 

660 orphans is expected given:
- Large archive folder (`yy_Archive/`)
- Many documentation files
- Draft files
- Media files

**This is normal. Focus on your writing, not linking every file.**

---

**Orphans are not a problem - many are expected! ✅**

```markdown
# CLI EXPLANATION - Do You Need It?
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## WHAT IS CLI?

**CLI = Command Line Interface**

It's a way to use programs by typing commands in the terminal instead of clicking buttons in a graphical app.

---

## DO YOU NEED ANY CLI?

**Short Answer: NO - You don't need any CLI for the MCP servers we set up!**

---

## WHAT WE SET UP

### ✅ **MCP Servers Configured (No CLI Needed):**

1. **DuckDuckGo Search** - ✅ Works immediately (uses `npx` automatically)
2. **Sequential Thinking** - ✅ Works immediately (uses `npx` automatically)  
3. **Walk This Way Files** - ✅ Works immediately (uses `npx` automatically)
4. **GitHub MCP** - ⚠️ Needs token (but NO CLI needed - just environment variable)

**All use `npx`** - This runs automatically, no CLI installation needed!

---

## WHAT ABOUT GITKRAKEN CLI?

### **GitKraken Desktop (What You Have):**
- ✅ You have GitKraken Desktop (the visual GUI)
- ✅ This is what you use for Git
- ✅ Works great, no CLI needed

### **GitKraken CLI (NOT Needed):**
- ❌ We mentioned GitKraken CLI earlier
- ❌ This was for GitKraken MCP server
- ❌ We **deferred** GitKraken MCP (not set up)
- ✅ **You don't need it** - GitKraken Desktop is enough

---

## GITHUB TOKEN SETUP (NOT a CLI)

For GitHub MCP, you need a **token**, not a CLI:

1. **Get GitHub Token:**
   - Go to: https://github.com/settings/tokens
   - Click "Generate new token (classic)"
   - Give it a name (e.g., "Cursor MCP")
   - Select scope: `repo` (for repository access)
   - Click "Generate token"
   - Copy the token (you'll only see it once!)

2. **Set Environment Variable:**
   ```bash
   export GITHUB_TOKEN="your_token_here"
   ```

3. **Make It Permanent (Optional):**
   ```bash
   echo 'export GITHUB_TOKEN="your_token_here"' >> ~/.zshrc
   source ~/.zshrc
   ```

**This is just a token/secret, not a CLI program.**

---

## SUMMARY

**Do You Need CLI?**
- ❌ **NO** - All MCP servers use `npx` (runs automatically)
- ❌ **NO** - GitKraken CLI not needed (GitKraken Desktop is enough)
- ⚠️ **GitHub Token** - Just a secret key, not a CLI program

**What Works Now:**
- ✅ DuckDuckGo MCP (works immediately)
- ✅ Sequential Thinking MCP (works immediately)
- ✅ Filesystem MCP (works immediately)
- ⚠️ GitHub MCP (needs token - but no CLI needed)

---

## BOTTOM LINE

**You don't need to install any CLI programs!**

Everything we set up uses `npx` which runs automatically. The only thing you might want is the GitHub token (which is just a secret key, not a program).

**You're all set! ✅**

```

You don't need to install any CLI.

## No CLI needed

The MCP servers we set up use `npx`, which runs automatically — no installation required.

### What "npx" means:
- It's a Node.js tool that downloads and runs packages automatically
- Cursor handles it — no manual setup
- The MCP servers (DuckDuckGo, Sequential Thinking, etc.) run via `npx`

### GitKraken CLI (not needed):
- Mentioned in earlier docs for GitKraken MCP
- We deferred GitKraken MCP, so you don't need it
- GitKraken Desktop is sufficient for your Git work

## What you have working now:
- DuckDuckGo Search — works immediately
- Sequential Thinking — works immediately
- Walk This Way Files — works immediately
- GitHub MCP — needs a token (not a CLI), just a secret key

## The only optional setup: GitHub token

If you want GitHub MCP to work, you need a GitHub token (not a CLI program). It's just a secret key:

1. Go to: https://github.com/settings/tokens
2. Generate a token
3. Set it as an environment variable (instructions in `.cursor/docs/MCP_SETUP_INSTRUCTIONS.md`)

But even this is optional — the other 3 MCP servers work without it.

## Bottom line:

No CLI programs to install. Everything uses `npx` automatically.

I created `.cursor/docs/CLI_EXPLANATION.md` with full details.

# CURSOR MCP & GITKRAKEN MCP STATUS
**Date:** January 10, 2026

## CURSOR MCP

### **What It Would Be:**
A hypothetical MCP server that exposes Cursor's own functionality (settings, extensions, workspace info, etc.) to the AI.

### **Status:**
❌ **Does Not Exist (As Far As We Know)**
- Cursor itself **uses** MCP servers (like we just set up)
- But there's no MCP server that exposes Cursor's internal functionality
- Cursor already has built-in AI features (Composer, Agents, etc.)
- No need for a "Cursor MCP" - Cursor IS the client

### **What You Have Instead:**
- ✅ Cursor's built-in AI features (Composer, Agents)
- ✅ MCP servers connected to Cursor (DuckDuckGo, GitHub, etc.)
- ✅ Agent system (HANK, JAZZ, CAROLINA, etc.)
- ✅ Memory system
- ✅ SmartWrite system

**Bottom Line:** You don't need a "Cursor MCP" - Cursor already has all the AI features built in!

---

## GITKRAKEN MCP

### **What It Is:**
An MCP server that gives AI access to GitKraken's git operations and repository context.

### **Status:**
⚠️ **Available But Requires Setup**

### **Requirements:**
- ✅ GitKraken Desktop (you have this - Pro subscription)
- ❌ GitKraken CLI (`gk` command) - **Required for MCP**
- ❌ MCP server setup

- Commit history analysis through AI
- Branch visualization queries
- Git operation automation via conversation
- Repository context for AI

   - Install the `gk` command-line tool
   - Free with your Pro subscription

2. **Authenticate:**
   ```bash
   gk auth login
   ```

3. **Configure MCP:**
   - Add to `~/.cursor/mcp.json`
   - Uses `gk mcp` command

### **Recommendation:**
**Deferred for Now:**
- You already have GitKraken Desktop (visual GUI)
- You have Git integration in Cursor
- GitKraken MCP is nice-to-have, not essential
- Can add later if you want AI-powered git operations

# GitKraken CLI Troubleshooting
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## CURRENT STATUS

✅ **GitKraken Desktop is installed** (found in `/Applications/`)
❌ **GitKraken CLI (`gk` command) is not yet accessible**

---

## IMPORTANT: CLI vs Desktop App

**GitKraken Desktop App** ≠ **GitKraken CLI**

The desktop app (GitKraken.app) is separate from the CLI tool (`gk`). The CLI needs to be installed separately.

---

## INSTALLATION OPTIONS

### **Option 1: Download Binary Directly**

1. Visit: https://www.gitkraken.com/cli
2. Download the macOS binary
3. Move it to a location in your PATH:
   ```bash
   # After downloading to ~/Downloads/gk
   chmod +x ~/Downloads/gk
   sudo mv ~/Downloads/gk /usr/local/bin/gk
   ```
4. Verify:
   ```bash
   gk --version
   ```

### **Option 2: Install Homebrew (Then Use Homebrew)**

If you want to use Homebrew (package manager for macOS):

```bash
# Install Homebrew first (if not installed)
/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Homebrew/install/HEAD/install.sh)"

# Then install GitKraken CLI
brew install gitkraken-cli
```

---

## WHERE ARE YOU STUCK?

**Please tell me:**
1. What did you try? (download, install, etc.)
2. What error message do you see?
3. Did you download a file? If so, where is it?

Once I know where you're stuck, I can help you complete the setup!

---

**Common Issues:**
- Downloaded binary but didn't move it to PATH
- Need to make binary executable (`chmod +x`)
- Need to authenticate after installation (`gk auth login`)

# GitKraken CLI Direct Install Instructions
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## YOUR MAC: Apple M4 Max (Apple Silicon)

✅ **You need: Apple Silicon / ARM64 version**

---

## EASIEST METHOD: Manual Download

Since Homebrew installation got complicated, let's do it manually:

### **Step 1: Download**

1. Visit: **https://github.com/gitkraken/gk-cli/releases**
2. Look for the latest release
3. Download the file that says:
   - `gk_Darwin_arm64.tar.gz` OR
   - `gk-macos-arm64.tar.gz` OR
   - Any file with "Darwin" or "macos" and "arm64"

### **Step 2: Extract**

After downloading, double-click the `.tar.gz` file to extract it (or use terminal)

### **Step 3: Install**

Once extracted, you'll have a `gk` file. Then run these commands in terminal:

```bash
# Make it executable
chmod +x ~/Downloads/gk

# Move it to a system directory
sudo mv ~/Downloads/gk /usr/local/bin/gk

# Verify it works
gk --version
```

### **Step 4: Authenticate**

```bash
gk auth login
```

---

# Fix "command not found: gk"
**Date:** January 10, 2026

---

## PROBLEM

You ran `gk auth login` but got:
```
zsh: command not found: gk
```

---

## CAUSE

We added `~/.local/bin` to your PATH in `~/.zshrc`, but your current terminal session hasn't reloaded it yet.

---

## SOLUTION

**Option 1: Reload your shell configuration (EASIEST)**

In your terminal, run:
```bash
source ~/.zshrc
```

Then try:
```bash
gk --version
gk auth login
```

---

**Option 2: Open a new terminal window**

Just open a fresh terminal window/tab, and `gk` should work automatically.

---

**Option 3: Use the full path (temporary)**

```bash
~/.local/bin/gk auth login
```

---

**Try Option 1 first - just run `source ~/.zshrc` in your terminal!**

```

Your terminal session needs to reload the PATH.

## Quick fix

In your terminal, run:
```bash
source ~/.zshrc
```

Then try:
```bash
gk --version
gk auth login
```

---

## What happened

We added `~/.local/bin` to your PATH in `~/.zshrc`, but your current terminal session hasn't loaded it yet. Running `source ~/.zshrc` reloads the configuration in the current session.

---

Alternative: you can also use the full path:
```bash
~/.local/bin/gk auth login
```

## When can I tell you about git operations?

Now. The MCP is active. I can:

## When do I expect a new GitKraken Desktop release?

I don’t have access to release schedules. That information isn’t available through the MCP. Check GitKraken’s official channels for release announcements.

## What’s most exciting in the GitKraken MCP?

1. Unified git operations: standard git commands plus GitKraken-specific features
2. Multi-provider support: GitHub, GitLab, Jira, Azure DevOps, Linear — all from one interface
3. Workspace management: list and manage GitKraken workspaces
4. Issue and PR integration: create, review, and comment on issues/PRs across providers
5. Repository file access: read files directly from remote repositories

## The “secret” for a GitKraken Pro user

The MCP lets you orchestrate git workflows from within Cursor. For example:
- Check status, create a branch, make changes, commit, and open a PR — all via chat
- Review PRs and add comments without leaving Cursor
- Access repository files across providers in one place

  "authors": {
    "Stephen King": {
      "source": "On Writing: A Memoir of the Craft",
      "core_philosophy": "Story is king. Character drives horror. Ordinary people in extraordinary situations. Accessible literary quality.",
      "principles": [
        {
          "topic": "writing_practice",
          "quote": "Read a lot and write a lot.",
          "explanation": "The only way to become a better writer is through consistent practice. Read widely across genres to understand what works, and write regularly to develop your craft."
        },
        {
          "topic": "adverbs",
          "quote": "The road to hell is paved with adverbs.",
          "explanation": "Adverbs often weaken prose. Instead of 'he said loudly,' use 'he shouted' or 'he bellowed.' Strong verbs are more effective than weak verbs with modifiers."
        },
        {
          "topic": "passive_voice",
          "quote": "The passive voice is weak and circuitous.",
          "explanation": "Use active voice for clarity and impact. 'The ball was hit by John' is weaker than 'John hit the ball.'"
        },
        {
          "topic": "character",
          "quote": "Character drives plot, not the other way around.",
          "explanation": "Let your characters' motivations and personalities drive the story. Don't force characters to serve plot; let plot emerge from character."
        },
        {
          "topic": "first_draft",
          "quote": "Write with the door closed, rewrite with the door open.",
          "explanation": "First draft is for you - write freely without judgment. Subsequent drafts are for readers - refine and polish with their experience in mind."
        },
        {
          "topic": "killing_darlings",
          "quote": "Kill your darlings, kill your darlings, even when it breaks your egocentric little scribbler's heart, kill your darlings.",
          "explanation": "Be willing to cut passages you love if they don't serve the story. Attachment to beautiful prose can weaken the overall narrative."
        },
        {
          "topic": "description",
          "quote": "Description begins in the writer's imagination, but should finish in the reader's.",
          "explanation": "Give readers enough detail to picture the scene, but leave room for their imagination. Over-description can distance readers from the story."
        },
        {
          "topic": "dialogue",
          "quote": "Dialogue is a skill best learned by people who enjoy talking and listening to others.",
          "explanation": "Good dialogue comes from observing how people actually speak. Listen to conversations, notice rhythms and patterns, and avoid exposition-heavy dialogue."
        },
        {
          "topic": "themes",
          "quote": "The themes in your work are like the bones in your body - you can't see them, but they hold everything together.",
          "explanation": "Themes should emerge naturally from story and character. Don't force themes; let them develop organically."
        },
        {
          "topic": "fear",
          "quote": "The scariest moment is always just before you start.",
          "explanation": "Beginning a writing project can be intimidating, but the fear diminishes once you begin. Start writing, even if it's imperfect."
        }
      ],
      "style_summary": {
        "prose_style": "Conversational, accessible, working/middle-class. No pretension. Vivid, often visceral metaphors grounded in physical/sensory.",
        "pacing": "Page-turner pacing. Knows when to speed up, slow down.",
        "narrative_technique": "Third person omniscient OR limited, often multiple POVs. Patient setup, then accelerating tension.",
        "character_voice": "Rich backstory. Natural, specific to character. Blue-collar authentic dialogue.",
        "themes": "Evil (supernatural and human), childhood trauma, small-town America, addiction and recovery, ordinary heroism"
      }
    },
    
    "Anne Lamott": {
      "source": "Bird by Bird: Some Instructions on Writing and Life",
      "core_philosophy": "Writing is a messy, imperfect process. Perfectionism is the enemy of creativity.",
      "principles": [
        {
          "topic": "first_draft",
          "quote": "The first draft is the child's draft, where you let it all pour out and then let it romp all over the place.",
          "explanation": "First drafts are meant to be messy. Give yourself permission to write badly in order to discover what you're really trying to say."
        },
        {
          "topic": "perfectionism",
          "quote": "Perfectionism is the voice of the oppressor, the enemy of the people.",
          "explanation": "Striving for perfection in early drafts prevents creativity. Allow yourself to write imperfectly, knowing you can revise later."
        },
        {
          "topic": "short_assignments",
          "quote": "You take it bird by bird.",
          "explanation": "Break overwhelming projects into small, manageable pieces. Focus on one small section at a time rather than the entire work."
        }
      ]
    },
    
    "Brandon Sanderson": {
      "source": "Writing Excuses podcast, lectures, blog posts",
      "core_philosophy": "Writing is a learnable craft. Understand the rules, then break them intentionally.",
      "principles": [
        {
          "topic": "worldbuilding",
          "quote": "Magic systems should have rules and limitations.",
          "explanation": "Internal consistency is crucial. Magic or fantastical elements need clear rules, even if those rules are simple, to maintain reader engagement and suspension of disbelief."
        },
        {
          "topic": "character",
          "quote": "Characters need to want something, even if it's only a glass of water.",
          "explanation": "Every scene needs character motivation. Characters must have clear desires that drive their actions, even in small scenes."
        },
        {
          "topic": "plot",
          "quote": "Plot is what happens. Story is what it means.",
          "explanation": "Plot is the sequence of events. Story is the emotional and thematic journey. Both are necessary, but story is what resonates with readers."
        }
      ]
    }
  },

      "key_elements": ["Suspense", "Plot structure", "Clues and red herrings", "Resolution"],
      "representative_authors": ["Dennis Lehane", "Thomas Harris", "Stephen King (some works)"]
    },
    "horror_supernatural": {
      "focus": "Atmosphere, dread, ordinary becoming sinister, character psychology",
      "pacing": "Patient setup, then accelerating tension",
      "key_elements": ["Atmosphere", "Dread building", "Character backstory", "Ordinary settings made sinister"],
      "representative_authors": ["Stephen King", "Thomas Harris"]
    },
    "coming_of_age": {
      "focus": "Character growth, authentic voice, period-specific details, emotional journey",
      "pacing": "Varies, often episodic",
      "key_elements": ["Authentic voice", "Character development", "Period details", "Emotional growth"],
      "representative_authors": ["J.D. Salinger", "Stephen Chbosky"]
    }
  },
  
  "quotes": {
    "stephen_king": [
      {
        "quote": "The scariest moment is always just before you start.",
        "context": "On beginning a writing project"
      },
      {
        "quote": "Read a lot and write a lot.",
        "context": "On developing craft"
      },
      {
        "quote": "The road to hell is paved with adverbs.",
        "context": "On prose style"
      },
      {
        "quote": "Kill your darlings, kill your darlings, even when it breaks your egocentric little scribbler's heart, kill your darlings.",
        "context": "On revision"
      },
      {
        "quote": "Description begins in the writer's imagination, but should finish in the reader's.",
        "context": "On description"
      }
    ],
    "general_writing": [
      {
        "quote": "Write with the door closed, rewrite with the door open.",
        "author": "Stephen King",
        "context": "On first drafts vs. revision"
      },
      {
        "quote": "Character drives plot, not the other way around.",
        "author": "Stephen King",
        "context": "On character and plot"
      },
      {
        "quote": "The first draft is the child's draft, where you let it all pour out.",
        "author": "Anne Lamott",
        "context": "On first drafts"
      },
      {
        "quote": "Characters need to want something, even if it's only a glass of water.",
        "author": "Brandon Sanderson",
        "context": "On character motivation"
      }
    ]
  }
}

Creating the MCP server with query tools:


```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
COMPASS Writing System - Writing Guides MCP Server
Comprehensive fiction writing reference database with author advice, techniques, and principles
"""

import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any
from mcp.server import Server
from mcp.types import Tool, TextContent
import mcp.server.stdio

# Initialize server
app = Server("compass-writing-guides")

# Data file path
DATA_DIR = Path(__file__).parent / "data"
GUIDES_FILE = DATA_DIR / "writing_guides.json"

def load_guides() -> dict:
    """Load writing guides database from JSON file"""
    if not GUIDES_FILE.exists():
        return {}
    with open(GUIDES_FILE, 'r') as f:
        return json.load(f)

@app.list_tools()
async def list_tools() -> list[Tool]:
    """List available writing guides tools"""
    return [
        Tool(
            name="get_author_advice",
            description="Get writing advice and principles from a specific author (e.g., 'Stephen King', 'Anne Lamott', 'Brandon Sanderson')",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "author": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Author name (e.g., 'Stephen King', 'Anne Lamott')"
                    },
                    "topic": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Optional: Filter by specific topic (e.g., 'adverbs', 'dialogue', 'character')"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["author"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_topic_guidance",
            description="Get comprehensive guidance on a writing topic (e.g., 'dialogue', 'pacing', 'character_development', 'prose_style', 'atmosphere', 'point_of_view', 'show_dont_tell', 'revision')",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "topic": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Writing topic name"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["topic"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_author_style",
            description="Get detailed author style guide (prose style, narrative technique, themes, signature moves, use cases). Available authors: Stephen King, Richard Russo, Dennis Lehane, Donna Tartt, J.D. Salinger, Annie Proulx, Cormac McCarthy, Colson Whitehead",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "author": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Author name"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["author"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_quote",
            description="Get inspirational writing quotes. Can filter by author or search by keyword",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "author": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Optional: Filter by author name"
                    },
                    "keyword": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Optional: Search for quotes containing keyword"
                    }
                },
                "required": []
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_technique",
            description="Get techniques for a specific aspect of writing (e.g., 'pacing_techniques', 'dialogue_techniques', 'character_techniques', 'prose_techniques', 'atmosphere_techniques')",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "technique_type": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Type of technique (e.g., 'pacing_techniques', 'dialogue_techniques')"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["technique_type"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_genre_guidance",
            description="Get genre-specific writing guidance (e.g., 'literary_fiction', 'crime_mystery', 'horror_supernatural', 'coming_of_age')",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "genre": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Genre name"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["genre"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="search_guides",
            description="Search across all writing guides (authors, topics, quotes, techniques) by keyword",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "query": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Search keyword or phrase"
                    }
                },
                "required": ["query"]
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="list_authors",
            description="List all available authors with writing advice",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {}
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="list_topics",
            description="List all available writing topics",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {}
            }
        ),
        Tool(
            name="get_writing_tip",
            description="Get a quick writing tip on a specific topic or from a specific author",
            inputSchema={
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {
                    "topic": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Optional: Topic (e.g., 'dialogue', 'pacing', 'character')"
                    },
                    "author": {
                        "type": "string",
                        "description": "Optional: Author name"
                    }
                },
                "required": []
            }
        )
    ]

@app.call_tool()
async def call_tool(name: str, arguments: Any) -> list[TextContent]:
    """Handle tool calls"""
    guides = load_guides()
    
    if not guides:
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({"error": "Writing guides database not found or empty"}, indent=2)
        )]
    
    if name == "get_author_advice":
        author_name = arguments.get("author", "")
        topic_filter = arguments.get("topic")
        
        authors = guides.get("authors", {})
        if author_name not in authors:
            available = list(authors.keys())
            return [TextContent(
                type="text",
                text=json.dumps({
                    "error": f"Author '{author_name}' not found",
                    "available_authors": available
                }, indent=2)
            )]
        
        author_data = authors[author_name]
        result = {
            "author": author_name,
            "source": author_data.get("source", ""),
            "core_philosophy": author_data.get("core_philosophy", ""),
            "principles": []
        }
        
        principles = author_data.get("principles", [])
        if topic_filter:
            principles = [p for p in principles if topic_filter.lower() in p.get("topic", "").lower()]
            result["filtered_by_topic"] = topic_filter
        
        result["principles"] = principles
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps(result, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_topic_guidance":
        topic_name = arguments.get("topic", "")
        
        topics = guides.get("topics", {})
        if topic_name not in topics:
            available = list(topics.keys())
            return [TextContent(
                type="text",
                text=json.dumps({
                    "error": f"Topic '{topic_name}' not found",
                    "available_topics": available
                }, indent=2)
            )]
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "topic": topic_name,
                **topics[topic_name]
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_author_style":
        author_name = arguments.get("author", "")
        
        styles = guides.get("author_styles", {})
        if author_name not in styles:
            available = list(styles.keys())
            return [TextContent(
                type="text",
                text=json.dumps({
                    "error": f"Author style '{author_name}' not found",
                    "available_authors": available
                }, indent=2)
            )]
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "author": author_name,
                **styles[author_name]
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_quote":
        author_filter = arguments.get("author", "")
        keyword = arguments.get("keyword", "")
        
        quotes = guides.get("quotes", {})
        results = []
        
        # Search in specific author quotes
        if author_filter:
            author_quotes = quotes.get(author_filter.lower().replace(" ", "_"), [])
            if keyword:
                author_quotes = [q for q in author_quotes if keyword.lower() in q.get("quote", "").lower() or keyword.lower() in q.get("context", "").lower()]
            results.extend([{**q, "author": author_filter} for q in author_quotes])
        # Search in general quotes
        else:
            general_quotes = quotes.get("general_writing", [])
            if keyword:
                general_quotes = [q for q in general_quotes if keyword.lower() in q.get("quote", "").lower() or keyword.lower() in q.get("context", "").lower()]
            results.extend(general_quotes)
            
            # Also search all author quotes if no author filter
            if not author_filter and not keyword:
                for author_key, author_quotes in quotes.items():
                    if author_key != "general_writing":
                        author_name = author_key.replace("_", " ").title()
                        results.extend([{**q, "author": author_name} for q in author_quotes])
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "quotes": results,
                "count": len(results)
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_technique":
        technique_type = arguments.get("technique_type", "")
        
        techniques = guides.get("techniques", {})
        if technique_type not in techniques:
            available = list(techniques.keys())
            return [TextContent(
                type="text",
                text=json.dumps({
                    "error": f"Technique type '{technique_type}' not found",
                    "available_techniques": available
                }, indent=2)
            )]
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "technique_type": technique_type,
                "techniques": techniques[technique_type]
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_genre_guidance":
        genre_name = arguments.get("genre", "")
        
        genres = guides.get("genre_guidance", {})
        if genre_name not in genres:
            available = list(genres.keys())
            return [TextContent(
                type="text",
                text=json.dumps({
                    "error": f"Genre '{genre_name}' not found",
                    "available_genres": available
                }, indent=2)
            )]
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "genre": genre_name,
                **genres[genre_name]
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "search_guides":
        query = arguments.get("query", "").lower()
        
        results = {
            "query": query,
            "matches": {
                "authors": [],
                "topics": [],
                "quotes": [],
                "techniques": []
            }
        }
        
        # Search authors
        for author_name, author_data in guides.get("authors", {}).items():
            author_str = json.dumps(author_data).lower()
            if query in author_str:
                results["matches"]["authors"].append(author_name)
        
        # Search topics
        for topic_name, topic_data in guides.get("topics", {}).items():
            topic_str = json.dumps(topic_data).lower()
            if query in topic_str:
                results["matches"]["topics"].append(topic_name)
        
        # Search quotes
        for author_key, quote_list in guides.get("quotes", {}).items():
            for quote_data in quote_list:
                quote_str = json.dumps(quote_data).lower()
                if query in quote_str:
                    results["matches"]["quotes"].append({
                        "quote": quote_data.get("quote", "")[:100] + "...",
                        "author": quote_data.get("author", author_key.replace("_", " ").title())
                    })
        
        # Search techniques
        for tech_type, tech_list in guides.get("techniques", {}).items():
            tech_str = " ".join(tech_list).lower()
            if query in tech_str:
                results["matches"]["techniques"].append(tech_type)
        
        total_matches = sum(len(v) for v in results["matches"].values())
        results["total_matches"] = total_matches
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps(results, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "list_authors":
        authors = guides.get("authors", {})
        author_styles = guides.get("author_styles", {})
        
        all_authors = list(set(list(authors.keys()) + list(author_styles.keys())))
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "authors": sorted(all_authors),
                "count": len(all_authors),
                "with_advice": list(authors.keys()),
                "with_styles": list(author_styles.keys())
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "list_topics":
        topics = guides.get("topics", {})
        
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "topics": list(topics.keys()),
                "count": len(topics),
                "descriptions": {k: v.get("description", "") for k, v in topics.items()}
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    elif name == "get_writing_tip":
        topic = arguments.get("topic", "")
        author = arguments.get("author", "")
        
        # If author specified, get a principle from that author
        if author:
            authors = guides.get("authors", {})
            if author in authors:
                principles = authors[author].get("principles", [])
                if topic:
                    principles = [p for p in principles if topic.lower() in p.get("topic", "").lower()]
                if principles:
                    selected = principles[0]
                    return [TextContent(
                        type="text",
                        text=json.dumps({
                            "tip": selected.get("quote", ""),
                            "explanation": selected.get("explanation", ""),
                            "author": author,
                            "topic": selected.get("topic", "")
                        }, indent=2)
                    )]
        
        # If topic specified, get guidance from that topic
        if topic:
            topics = guides.get("topics", {})
            if topic in topics:
                topic_data = topics[topic]
                principles = topic_data.get("principles", [])
                if principles:
                    return [TextContent(
                        type="text",
                        text=json.dumps({
                            "tip": principles[0] if isinstance(principles[0], str) else principles[0].get("principle", ""),
                            "topic": topic,
                            "description": topic_data.get("description", "")
                        }, indent=2)
                    )]
        
        # Default: return a general tip
        return [TextContent(
            type="text",
            text=json.dumps({
                "tip": "Read a lot and write a lot. - Stephen King",
                "explanation": "The only way to become a better writer is through consistent practice. Read widely across genres to understand what works, and write regularly to develop your craft.",
                "author": "Stephen King"
            }, indent=2)
        )]
    
    return [TextContent(
        type="text",
        text=json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown tool: {name}"}, indent=2)
    )]

async def main():
    """Run the MCP server"""
    async with mcp.server.stdio.stdio_server() as (read_stream, write_stream):
        await app.run(
            read_stream,
            write_stream,
            app.create_initialization_options()
        )

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import asyncio
    asyncio.run(main())

```

mcp
